- The audit log records creating and revoking a link, with its ID only.

**Recipient page.** `GET /s/{id}` serves one static page embedded in `hisar-server`, the same bytes for every ID: serving it looks nothing up, counts nothing and reveals nothing.

- No third-party code. Its script and style are inlined and allowed by their SHA-256 hashes: `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; script-src 'sha256-…'; style-src 'sha256-…'; connect-src 'self'; img-src data:; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'`, with `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` and `X-Frame-Options: DENY`.
- **Reveal first.** The page reads the key from the fragment but fetches nothing until the recipient clicks **Reveal**, so link previews in chat apps, mail scanners and crawlers, which load the URL without clicking, never use up a view. Reveal fetches the ciphertext (one view), asks for the passphrase when the link has one, and decrypts in the browser with WebCrypto. Once the server has answered with the ciphertext, the page replaces its address with one without the fragment (`history.replaceState`), so the tab's back and forward history and a restored tab no longer hold the key; the browser's list of visited pages may still have the address as it was opened.
- A wrong passphrase keeps the ciphertext in the page, so another try costs no view ("That passphrase doesn't open this link."). A missing or malformed key says the link is incomplete; a 404 says "This link has expired or was already viewed", without saying which.
- It shows the title and each field with **Copy** (concealed fields hidden until **Show**), websites as text, notes as preformatted text, and when the link expires and how many views are left, or "That was the last view: this link no longer works". Light and dark follow the system; it works on phones, with the keyboard and with screen readers (labelled controls, live status messages).
- **Trust.** The page comes from the server that stores the ciphertext, so a compromised server could serve a page that sends the key elsewhere. That is the limit of every web-based share link; recipients with `hisar` can open links without the page (`hisar share-link open`, below), and opening them in the desktop and iPhone apps is a follow-up.
- The footer says "Shared with Hisar, the end-to-end encrypted password, SSH key and secrets manager for developers. Nothing you see here is stored by this page." without a link: there is no public product page to link to yet.

**Desktop app.**

- The item view has **Share link…** next to Edit (not for SSH keys without notes or fields). Its dialog offers the fields to share as checkboxes (username and password checked by default), **Expires** (1 hour, 1 day, 7 days, 30 days; 7 days by default), **Views** (1–100, 1 by default, or Unlimited) and an optional passphrase. **Create link** shows the link with **Copy** (through the backend, cleared after 90 s) and what it shares.
- The item view lists that item's live links. **Shared links** in the sidebar, under the vaults, lists all of them; the new item menu has **Share text…**. Each link shows its title, what it shares, views used, expiry and whether it needs a passphrase, with **Copy link** and **Revoke**.
- Lists refresh on `share-links-changed`. Offline, creating and revoking are refused like other changes.

**CLI.** `hisar share-link <item> [-v vault] [--field name]... [--expires 1h|1d|7d|30d] [--views 1-100|unlimited] [--passphrase]` prints the link on stdout (by default username and password, 7 days, 1 view); `--field` is `username`, `password`, `url`, `notes` or a custom field's label, and `--passphrase` asks for it twice. `hisar share-link --text [--title t]` shares text read from stdin. `hisar share-links` lists the live links and `hisar share-link revoke <id>` revokes one. `hisar share-link open <url>` opens any share link in the terminal, without an account and without the web page: it counts a view, asks for the passphrase when the link has one, decrypts with `core/sharelink` and prints the title and fields. Through the app, creating a link always opens the approval dialog, even in an approved terminal session, because it hands secrets to people outside Hisar: "hisar in Terminal wants to create a share link for “GitHub”", with the expiry, the views and the fields; approving it also approves the terminal session. Standalone, the commands ask for the master password like the others.

### 5.10 Renaming, deleting and leaving vaults

Owners rename and delete vaults; anyone who was given a vault can leave it. The server checks every rule again and never sees a vault name or an item.

**Rename (owner).** Vault names are encrypted with `K_vault` (§4.4), so the owner's client seals the new name (`crypt.SealVaultName`, `session.RenameVault`) and sends only the ciphertext: `PUT /vaults/{id}/name {encName, keyGeneration}`, 204, 403 for anyone but an owner, 404 for a non-member, 409 when `keyGeneration` is not the vault's current one (required for organization vaults, checked for any vault when set), so a name sealed with a key a rotation replaced is never stored. Every member gets `vaults-changed` and shows the new name on its next sync. A Personal vault can be renamed too. Secret references (§9.4.1) that name the old name stop resolving, which the CLI says after a rename.

**Delete (owner).** `POST /vaults/{id}/delete {moveToVaultId, moveToKeyGeneration, movedItems, deleteItems}`; one transaction locks the vault's organization, when it has one, then the vault and the target in ID order (§5.11), and checks, in this order:

- the caller is an owner (403), the vault is not a Personal vault (400 "personal vaults can't be deleted": it is the vault Hisar keeps for the user alone, and logins saved from the browser and the user's phone go there), and it is not the caller's only vault (400);
- with `moveToVaultId`: the target is another vault the caller can write to (owner or editor; 403 otherwise, 404 for a non-member), `moveToKeyGeneration` is the generation of the target's key the items are sealed with (required when the target is an organization vault, 400 without it as for item writes, §7.3, and checked for any target when set; 409 `{error, keyChanged: true}` for another generation, and nothing is moved or deleted: the client reloads the target, seals the items again with its current key and retries once, §7.3), and `movedItems` are exactly the vault's live items at their current revisions (409 otherwise, so a concurrent edit is never lost; the client re-reads and retries up to 3 times). Each moved item keeps its item ID, gets a new revision and the target's next `seq`, and a history entry in the target; it replaces a tombstone with its ID in the target (an item that once moved out of the target comes back), while a live item with its ID there refuses the move with 409. The client (`session.DeleteVault`) decrypts each item with the source vault's key and seals it again with the target's key, vault ID and the new revision in the AAD (§4.5), so the server only copies ciphertext;
- without a move, a vault with live items needs `deleteItems: true` (409 otherwise). The apps only send it after the user typed the vault's name.

Then it deletes the vault row; access grants, wrapped keys, items, tombstones, item history and share invitations go with it (`ON DELETE CASCADE`). It answers 204, records `vault_deleted`, sends `vaults-changed` to every former member and `vault-changed` to the target's members. Requests may be as large as a key rotation (32 MiB, at most 50 000 items).

**Leave (member).** `DELETE /vaults/{id}/access/{own account ID}` removes the caller's access and wrapped key (`store.LeaveVault`, one transaction). An owner may leave only while another owner remains (400 otherwise: "delete it, or make someone else an owner first"); a Personal vault has one owner and cannot be left. Leaving does not rotate the vault key: only owners can rotate, and the items the leaver already downloaded stay readable to them anyway, as after a removal (§4.4). The remaining members get `vaults-changed`; the audit log records `vault_left`.

**Who loses access.** Before deleting a shared vault the apps list its other members with their roles (`GetVaultActions`; never anyone else, §5.8). Their apps drop the vault on their next sync: listing vaults replaces the cached list (§9.1), and `session.Session` compares it with the cached one first. A vault that disappeared is reported once, with its name decrypted from the cache while the cached wrapped key still opens it (`TakeAllRemovedVault`), except the vaults this session deleted or left itself. The Mac and the iPhone show "“Work” is no longer available: it was deleted, or you no longer have access to it." above the vault list; the server does not say which, so neither does the notice. A device without a cache of that vault (signed in later) shows nothing.

**Apps.** `core/app` has `GetVaultActions` (whether the user may rename, delete or leave, the reason when not, the members who lose access and the vaults its items can move to), `RenameVault`, `DeleteVault` (move, or delete with items after the typed name; the backend checks the name again), `LeaveVault` and `ListRemovedVaults`, used by the Mac, the iPhone (through `mobile/hisarkit`) and `hisar` (§9.4). Deleting or leaving also turns the vault's "Available to agents" switch off (§9.4.2).

**CLI.** `hisar vault rename <vault> [name]`, `hisar vault delete <vault> [--move-to <vault> | --delete-items --confirm <vault name>] [-y]`, `hisar vault leave <vault> [-y]` and `hisar vault create <name>` (`vault-create` stays). In a terminal they ask what is missing: the new name; for a delete, the item count, "These people lose access:" with each member's name, email and role, then "What happens to its items?" with one "Move them to <vault>" line per writable vault (1, the default) and "Delete them with the vault", which asks "Type Work to delete it and its N items"; a move or an empty vault asks "(y/N)". Without a terminal nothing is asked: a vault with items needs `--move-to` or `--delete-items --confirm <name>`, and a move, an empty vault or a leave needs `-y`. A name typed or passed that is not the vault's exact name deletes nothing. Through the app the commands use `vaults.actions`, `vaults.rename`, `vaults.delete` and `vaults.leave` (§9.4); standalone they run the same session code.

**Organization vaults.** Members get an organization vault through their teams (§5.11), so it cannot be left, and nobody is removed from it directly: `DELETE /vaults/{id}/access/{accountID}` answers 400 "access to an organization vault comes from its teams" for it, and `GetVaultActions` says to ask an organization admin instead. Owners rename and delete it as any other vault; a delete that moves items may move them into, or out of, an organization vault.

### 5.11 Organizations, teams and organization vaults

An organization is a group of accounts, for example a company, that shares vaults through teams instead of person by person. Its admins manage who is in it, its teams and which teams get which vaults; they never get a vault key by being admins. An admin who adds themselves to a team gets its vaults from an owner's app like any other member, which every member sees in the team and the audit log records. Owners' apps deliver keys to whoever the server says is in a team (§2.2), verifying and pinning each recipient's keys as for a share. There is no admin recovery (§6) and no billing, SSO or policy.

**Model.**

- **Organization:** a name (server-visible, §2.3) and members, each `admin` or `member`. Any signed-in user creates one with `POST /organizations {name}` and is its first admin. An organization always keeps at least one admin.
- **Team:** a name, unique in its organization regardless of case, and a subset of the organization's members. Only admins create, rename and delete teams and add or remove their members.
- **Organization vault:** a vault of type `organization` with `organizationId`. It is given to one or more teams of that organization, each with a vault role (`owner`, `editor`, `reader`). Organization and team names are 1 to 100 characters without control characters (`apitypes.CleanOrganizationName`).
- **Entitlement:** an account is entitled to an organization vault while it is in at least one team given that vault; its role is the strongest of those teams' roles (`apitypes.RankVaultRole`). Every `vault_access` row of an organization vault belongs to an entitled account and has its entitled role; an entitled account without a row has no wrapped key yet and is a **key request**. One store function (`reconcileOrganizationVaults`) keeps this true: it runs at the end of every transaction that changes organization members, team members, team vaults or teams, deletes the rows of accounts that are no longer entitled and marks those vaults `needs_rotation`, and sets every other row's role to the entitled role.
- **Invariants**, checked by the reconciliation: every vault keeps at least one `vault_access` row with role `owner`, so somebody can always deliver keys and rotate; a change that would leave an organization vault without one is refused with 409 "Nobody who can open this vault would be its owner. Give owner access to a team whose members already have the vault first." (Account deletion is the exception and follows §5.7.1.) An organization vault has at most 1000 entitled accounts, the most one rotation carries (409 otherwise).
- **Serialization.** Every transaction that changes an organization, or the access of one of its vaults (key delivery, rotation, creating or deleting an organization vault), first locks the organization's row (`SELECT … FOR UPDATE`), then the vault rows it touches in ID order, and only then checks rights, invariants and entitlements; item writes and moves lock only their vault rows, in ID order, since they change no access, so they wait for a rotation of the same vault but not for the rest of the organization. Concurrent changes to one organization therefore run one after the other, and no check is made against a state another transaction is changing.
- **Key generation.** Each vault has `key_generation`, 1 at creation, incremented by every rotation, and listed as the vault's `keyGeneration`, so a key wrapped before a rotation is never delivered after it, and a name (§5.10) or an item sealed before one is never stored: item writes, item moves and vault deletions that move items carry the generation of the key their items are sealed with, check it while holding the vault rows a rotation locks too, and are refused otherwise (§7.3).

**Organization vaults and keys.**

1. **Create.** A member of a team creates a vault for it: `POST /vaults {id, type: "organization", organizationId, teamId, encName, wrappedKey}`, with the key wrapped to the creator as for any vault (§4.4). The team is given the vault as `owner` and the creator gets an owner row (403 when the caller is not in the team, 404 for a team of another organization). The other members of the team are key requests.
2. **Give a vault to a team.** Admins: `PUT /organizations/{org}/teams/{team}/vaults/{vault} {role}` gives or changes, `DELETE` takes it back; the vault must belong to the organization (404 otherwise). Roles change at once on the server; nothing is re-wrapped for a role change.
3. **Deliver keys.** `GET /vault-key-requests` returns `pending`: the key requests of the organization vaults the caller owns, each with the vault's `keyGeneration` and the recipient's account ID, name, email and signed public keys, and `waiting`: the organization vaults the caller is entitled to and has no key for, so the app can say it waits for an owner. On every sync, and at once on `vaults-changed` or `organizations-changed`, an owner's app (`session.CompleteVaultKeyRequests`) verifies each recipient's signed KEM key, pins their signing key (§5.6), wraps `K_vault` to them and calls `PUT /vaults/{id}/access/{accountID} {wrappedKey, role, keyGeneration}` with the generation of the key it wrapped. For an organization vault, in one transaction under the organization lock, the server accepts a new member only while the account is entitled, `role` is its entitled role and `keyGeneration` is the vault's current one (409 otherwise: the app syncs and tries again on its next sync), and for an existing member only a new wrapped key with the same role and the current generation (400 "roles in an organization vault come from its teams" for a different role). A shared vault checks `keyGeneration` too when the request carries it. Until an owner's app is unlocked and online the recipient waits, as for an accepted share (§5.8).
4. **Rotate.** A vault marked `needs_rotation` (the vault list's `needsRotation`) is rotated by the next owner's app that syncs (`session.RotatePendingVaults`), with every current member and role, exactly as §4.4. For an organization vault the submitted members must be exactly the current `vault_access` rows with their current roles (400 otherwise), not a subset as when an owner removes someone from a shared vault, and the request must name the vault's current `keyGeneration` (409 otherwise, so a second owner rotating an empty vault from the same key cannot replace the first one's key). The rotation increments `key_generation` and clears `needs_rotation`. Until then the server already refuses everything to the removed account: it has no access row.

**Invitations.** Joining an organization needs no key, so it is one step:

- `POST /organizations/{org}/invitations {email, role}` (admins) answers `202 {"message": "Invitation sent"}` whether or not the email has an account, and works like a share invitation (§5.8): one per organization and email (a repeat replaces the role and expiry), 7 days, `organizations-changed` and an email to an existing account, a sign-up invite (§5.1) for any other address. Only the admin's own data is reported: their own email (400) or an existing member (409). It shares the share-invitation rate limits (50 per account and 100 per IP address per day).
- `GET /organizations` returns the caller's organizations (`id`, `name`, `role`, `memberCount`) and `invitations`: the unexpired invitations to the caller's email, with the organization's name, the inviter's name and email and the role. `POST /organization-invitations/{id}/accept` makes the caller a member at once and deletes the invitation; `/decline` deletes it. `DELETE /organizations/{org}/invitations/{id}` (admins) withdraws one.

**Members and teams.** `GET /organizations/{org}` (members only; 404 for anyone else) returns the organization, the caller's role, every member (account ID, name, email, role, signed public keys), every team with its member IDs and vaults (`vaultId`, `role`) and, for admins only, the pending invitations. Members of an organization see each other this way; nobody else does, and there is still no list of every account (§5.8).

| Change | Endpoint | Who |
|---|---|---|
| Rename, delete organization | `PUT /organizations/{org}/name {name}`, `DELETE /organizations/{org}` | admins; delete is refused (400) while the organization has vaults |
| Change a member's role | `PUT /organizations/{org}/members/{accountID} {role}` | admins; never the last admin (400) |
| Remove a member, leave | `DELETE /organizations/{org}/members/{accountID}` | admins remove others; anyone removes themselves, except the last admin while others remain and the last member (400: delete the organization instead) |
| Teams | `POST /organizations/{org}/teams {name}`, `PUT …/teams/{team}/name {name}`, `DELETE …/teams/{team}` | admins |
| Team members | `PUT`, `DELETE /organizations/{org}/teams/{team}/members/{accountID}` | admins; the account must be a member of the organization (404) |

Removing someone from the organization removes them from its teams. Every change that touches entitlements runs the reconciliation in the same transaction, under the organization lock, so it can be refused by the invariants (409).

**Events and audit.** Every change sends `organizations-changed` to the organization's members (and to the invited account for invitations); a change to entitlements also sends `vaults-changed` to the accounts whose access changed and to the owners of each affected vault, whose apps then deliver keys or rotate. The audit log records `organization_created`, `organization_renamed`, `organization_deleted`, `organization_invitation_sent|accepted|declined|cancelled`, `organization_member_role_changed`, `organization_member_removed`, `organization_left`, `team_created`, `team_renamed`, `team_deleted`, `team_member_added`, `team_member_removed`, `team_vault_granted` and `team_vault_revoked`.

**Account deletion (§5.7.1).** The account's vaults follow §5.7.1 unchanged, organization vaults included: one with no other owner is deleted with the account. Its memberships and team memberships go; when it was an organization's last admin, the remaining member who joined earliest becomes admin, and an organization left with no members is deleted with its teams and invitations. The organization invitations it sent are withdrawn, and those to its email are deleted; the remaining members of its organizations, the recipients of the withdrawn invitations and the members of every organization that had an invitation from or to it get `organizations-changed`. `created_by`, `added_by` and `granted_by` keep the deleted account's ID, without a foreign key, as `vaults.created_by` does (§5.7.1). Every organization vault that stays and that the account held a key to is marked `needs_rotation` in the same transaction, without the reconciliation's owner check, so its owners' apps rotate it as after a team removal; shared vaults are not marked, as in §5.7.1. The deletion preview lists the organizations (`organizations`: ID, name, `deleted` for those that go with the account, and `newAdminName` when another member becomes admin), and counts the organization invitations it sent and received in `sentInvitationCount` and `receivedInvitationCount`.

**Apps.** `core/app` completes key requests and pending rotations on every sync, next to accepted shares (§5.8), and has the organization calls for the UI; the iPhone reaches them through `mobile/hisarkit`. **CLI:** `hisar orgs` lists organizations and invitations; `hisar org create|show|rename|delete|invite|cancel|accept|decline|role|remove|leave`; `hisar team create|rename|delete|add|remove`; `hisar vault create <name> --org <org> --team <team>`; `hisar vault give|take <vault> --org <org> --team <team> [-r role]`. `hisar orgs` and `hisar org show` also deliver pending keys and rotate pending vaults, and say so.

**Screens (Mac and iPhone, the same model and copy, docs/COPY.md "Organizations").**

- **Organizations** in Settings (Mac: a source-list section after People, with the number of invitations for you as a badge; iPhone: a Settings row that pushes the list). Top to bottom: **Invitations for you** ("Ada invited you to Acme as a member", the inviter's email, **Decline** and **Accept**); the organizations with your role and member count; **Create organization…** (a name, 1–100 characters). A received invitation also shows as a banner above the vault list, like a share invitation (§5.8), with the same two buttons.
- **Organization** (Mac: the section's detail pane after selecting one; iPhone: a pushed screen). The name and your role. **Members**: name, email, role (Admin or Member), security code, and the key-changed warning (§5.6); **Teams**: each team with its members and its vaults (the vault's name, or "A vault you can't open" when this device has no key for it) and each vault's role. Admins also get: **Invite by email** (email, role Member or Admin, **Send invitation**; every address gets the same "Invitation sent"), **Invited** (pending invitations with **Cancel**), per member **Make admin** / **Make member** and **Remove from Acme…**, **New team…**, per team **Rename…**, **Delete team…**, **Add member** (a picker of the organization's members not yet in the team), **Remove** per team member, **Give a vault…** (a picker of the organization's vaults, by name where this device can open them, and a role) and **Take back** per vault, **Rename organization…** and **Delete organization…** (refused with the server's reason while it has vaults). Everyone gets **Leave Acme…**. A refusal shows the server's sentence (for example the owner invariant, §5.11 Model) in an alert.
- **Vault list.** Organization vaults are grouped under their organization's name (Mac sidebar: a heading per organization after the other vaults; iPhone Vaults screen: a section per organization). The vault's role works as for any vault (§5.8): readers cannot edit, editors and owners can.
- **New vault.** The new-vault dialog (Mac) and screen (iPhone) have **Where**: "Shared vault" (the current behavior) or one entry per team the user is in ("Acme › Platform"); a team entry creates an organization vault for that team (`CreateOrganizationVault`), and the footer says the team's members get it and it is managed in Settings → Organizations.
- **An organization vault's sharing panel (Mac) and sheet (iPhone)** replace **Invite by email** and the member list with "Shared through teams in Acme": each team that has the vault, its role and its members. Admins get **Give to a team…** and **Take back** there; everyone else reads "Ask an admin of Acme to change who has this vault." The vault's **Leave vault…** shows core's reason (§5.10).
- **Waiting for a key.** For each organization with vaults the user is entitled to without a key (`ListWaitingVaultKeys`), the vault list shows one line, "Waiting for an owner of a vault in Acme to give you its key" or "Waiting for the owners of 2 vaults in Acme to give you their keys", until the vaults arrive; a key Hisar could not deliver or a rotation it could not do shows as the vault list's warning, like a share that could not be completed (§5.8). An organization vault is never labelled "shared".
- **Account deletion** (§5.7.1) lists the organizations the account is in: "Acme is deleted with your account" or "Ada becomes an admin of Acme".

## 6. Account recovery

Recovery is only through the Emergency Kit (§4.1). With the email, server and Secret Key printed on it, plus their master password, a user signs in on any device (§5.3). There is no other path: no admin recovery, no recovery links, no trusted contacts.

- **Lost Secret Key:** it is on the Emergency Kit, and on every device still signed in, where the desktop app saves a new Emergency Kit after the master password (Settings → Security → Emergency Kit) and the CLI has `hisar emergency-kit`. A Secret Key that may have leaked is replaced from a signed-in device (§5.7).
- **Forgotten master password:** it cannot be recovered by anyone, including Hisar. The server never sees it, and nothing the server or a device stores opens the keyset without it, so the data only that account can open is lost; shared vaults stay with their other members. The apps say so plainly when the account is created and on the Secret Key / Emergency Kit screen.
- Losing the Emergency Kit and every signed-in device loses the account as well.

**Admin recovery was removed.** Earlier versions let one admin hold a recovery keyset that every recoverable vault key was wrapped to, and recover other users' accounts with one-time links. Migration `00004_remove_recovery` deletes all of it: the tables `recovery_keyset`, `vault_recovery`, `recovery_tokens` and `account_recovery` and the column `vaults.recovery_opt_out`. Its down migration recreates only the empty structure; the deleted keyset, wrappings and pending recoveries cannot come back. The recovery endpoints are gone and answer 404. Older clients keep working: a 404 for the recovery keyset means "recovery is not set up" to them, so they stop wrapping vault keys to it.

## 7. Sync protocol

### 7.1 Model

- Each vault has a monotonic `seq` counter on the server.
- Each item has an `id` (UUIDv7, client generated), a `revision` (UUIDv7, client generated per edit), a `seq` (assigned by the server on write) and a `deleted` flag (tombstone).
- The server stores the current version and keeps item versions as history for 90 days.

### 7.2 Pull

```
GET /api/v1/vaults/{vaultID}/changes?since={seq}&limit=500
→ { items: [ {id, revision, seq, deleted, overview, details, updatedAt, updatedBy} ], nextSeq, more }
```

A page ends after `limit` items or once its sealed overviews and details reach the soft 16 MiB ciphertext budget. The query checks the total before each item, so one item can cross the budget and the first item always fits. Current per-item limits keep ciphertext below 17.0625 MiB. Base64 and bounded JSON metadata keep the response below the apps' 64 MiB read limit. When a page ends at either bound, it answers `more: true` and `nextSeq` is its last item's `seq`.

The client applies changes to its local SQLite cache and stores `nextSeq`. Changes arrive in two ways: server push, and polling (every 60 s and on focus) as a fallback.

**Push (server-sent events).** `GET /api/v1/events` (session required) is a `text/event-stream` that tells clients when to sync. It carries no vault data, only IDs and sequence numbers.

- `event: vault-changed` with `{"vaultID", "seq"}` goes to every member of a vault after an item is written (with the new `seq`) or the key is rotated (`seq` 0).
- `event: vaults-changed` with `{}` tells an account that its list of vaults changed: it was given or lost access, created a vault, or was part of a vault before a rotation, a rename or a deletion, or that someone left it (§5.10).
- `event: share-invitations-changed` with `{}` tells an account that its received or sent share invitations changed (§5.8): one was sent to it or withdrawn, or one it can complete was accepted or declined. The desktop backend then completes accepted shares and refreshes the UI.
- `event: organizations-changed` with `{}` tells an account that an organization it belongs to changed (members, roles, teams, team vaults, name) or that an invitation to one was sent to it or withdrawn (§5.11).
- `event: share-links-changed` with `{}` tells an account that one of its share links was created, viewed, used up or revoked (§5.9).
- `event: account-preferences-changed` with `{}` tells an account that one of its devices saved the preferences record, such as a new favorite (§4.7).
- `event: approval-requested`, `approval-answered` and `approval-resolved` with `{"requestId"}` go to every stream of the account for phone approvals (§9.9). They carry no request or answer, only the ID.
- The server sends a `: heartbeat` comment every 25 s and re-checks the stream's session each time, so logging out or revoking the device ends the stream.
- Each write pushes the connection's write deadline forward, so the server's 30 s response write timeout does not cut the stream.
- An account may hold 8 streams. A subscriber whose 32-event buffer fills is closed, so the client reconnects and resyncs.
- The broker lives in one server process, which is what the deployment runs; several processes would need Postgres `LISTEN/NOTIFY`.
- The desktop backend keeps one stream open while unlocked. Its client has no overall request timeout, but closes a stream that sends nothing, not even a heartbeat, for 90 s. It reconnects with backoff from 1 s to 60 s, and stops on 401.
- The iOS app runs the same backend. iOS suspends it in the background, after which the old stream can be dead without the client noticing for up to 90 s, and the backoff may have grown to 60 s. So on returning to the foreground the app calls `ReconnectEvents`, which drops the stream and opens a new one at once with the backoff reset.
- On an event the backend syncs every vault incrementally (bursts collapse into one sync), so the SSH agent and the browser bridge see the change even when the window is hidden. It then emits `remote-change`, and the UI runs its background refresh.

### 7.3 Push

```
PUT /api/v1/vaults/{vaultID}/items/{itemID}
If-Match: <base revision>    (absent for new items)
{ revision, overview, details, deleted, keyGeneration }
→ 200 { seq } | 409 { current item } | 409 { error, keyChanged: true } | 400 { error }
```

On `409 { current item }` the client merges (§7.4) and retries with the current revision as the new base. A tombstone (`deleted: true`) replaces an existing item, so one without `If-Match` is refused with 400 "invalid item".

`keyGeneration` is the generation of the vault key the item is sealed with (§5.11): required for organization vaults and checked for any vault when set, so older clients keep writing to personal and shared vaults. In the write's transaction, after locking the vault and before comparing revisions, the server refuses another generation with `409 { error, keyChanged: true }` and stores nothing, so an item sealed with a key a rotation replaced never reaches the vault, where nobody with the current key could open it. The client (`session.SaveItem`, `DeleteItem`, `MoveItem` and `DeleteVault`) then reloads the vault, seals the item again with the current key and retries once. A rotation also gives every item a new revision, so an edit, a deletion or a move out of that vault retries with the revision the rotation wrote when the item's content is still the plaintext the edit started from. When someone really changed it, the retry is a conflict (§7.4) and the edit stays with the user. If the key-changed refusal comes back, the client returns "the vault's key changed on another device; try again" and the edit stays with the user, so trying again saves it. An item move carries the source vault's generation in `tombstone.keyGeneration` and the target's in `moved.keyGeneration`.

An item write, an item move or a vault deletion's move that names no generation for an organization vault is refused with `400 { error: "update Hisar to change items in organization vaults" }` and stores nothing. Only apps older than key generations send none, and they read any `409` without a current item as an item conflict, asking for a reload that never helps; a `400` they show as it is. Only a nonzero stale generation, which only newer apps send, gets the key-changed `409`.

### 7.4 Conflict resolution (client side)

The server cannot read items, so merging happens on the client:

1. Three-way merge per field using the local base version (from local history).
2. If the same field changed on both sides: keep the remote value as current and the local value in the item's field history, and show a "conflict resolved" badge.
3. If the merge cannot be done (e.g. one side deleted): keep both — restore the item and add a "(conflicted copy)" item.

### 7.5 Rollback and withholding detection

- Clients remember the highest `seq` seen per vault. A response with a lower `seq` is rejected as a rollback.
- A server can still hide recent writes from other devices. This is accepted because we run the server.

## 8. Server

### 8.1 Data model (Postgres)

```
accounts       (id, email, name, role[admin|member], suspended_at, argon2_params, salt, opaque_record,
                enc_keyset, x25519_pub, ed25519_pub, x25519_sig, created_at)
devices        (id, account_id, name, platform, created_at, last_seen_at, revoked_at)
sessions       (token_hash, account_id, device_id, expires_at)
invites        (token_hash, email, role, created_by, created_at, expires_at, used_at)
share_links    (id, account_id, ciphertext, encrypted_label, passphrase_salt, passphrase_iterations,
                max_views, view_count, created_at, expires_at)
share_invitations (id, vault_id, email, role, invited_by, state[pending|accepted], accepted_by,
                created_at, expires_at, accepted_at)   -- unique (vault_id, email)
vaults         (id, type[personal|shared|organization], organization_id, enc_name, seq, key_generation, needs_rotation, created_at)
vault_access   (vault_id, account_id, wrapped_key, role[owner|editor|reader], granted_by)
organizations  (id, name, created_by, created_at)
organization_members (organization_id, account_id, role[admin|member], added_by, added_at)
organization_invitations (id, organization_id, email, role, invited_by, created_at, expires_at)   -- unique (organization_id, email)
teams          (id, organization_id, name, created_by, created_at)   -- unique (organization_id, lower(name))
team_members   (team_id, organization_id, account_id, added_by, added_at)   -- the account is a member of the organization
team_vaults    (team_id, vault_id, organization_id, role[owner|editor|reader], granted_by, granted_at)   -- the vault is the organization's
items          (vault_id, id, revision, seq, deleted, overview_ct, details_ct, updated_at, updated_by)
item_history   (vault_id, id, revision, overview_ct, details_ct, updated_at, updated_by)
attachments    (id, vault_id, item_id, size, chunk_count, storage_path)
audit_events   (id, account_id, device_id, type, detail, ip, created_at)   -- kept 1 year (§8.6); indexed by time, account and type
account_preferences (account_id, revision, ciphertext, updated_at)   -- sealed with K_user (§4.7)
instance_settings (id = 1, settings jsonb, updated_at, updated_by)   -- one row, the settings admins save (§8.3)
```

`enc_name` is the vault name encrypted with `K_vault`.

Phone approvals (§9.9) are not in Postgres: they live in the server process's memory for at most 4 minutes, like the event broker.

Session lookups slide expiry before recording `devices.last_seen_at` in a separate statement. A failed activity write is
logged without failing authentication. No session row lock is held while updating a device. Transactions that lock
both devices and their sessions must lock devices first, matching device revocation.

### 8.2 API surface (v1)

| Area | Endpoints |
|---|---|
| Auth | `POST /auth/register/{start,finish}`, `POST /auth/login/{start,finish}`, `POST /auth/logout`, `GET /auth/prelogin?email=` (salt, params) |
| Account | `GET /account`, `POST /account/reauthenticate`, `PUT /account/credentials` (§5.7), `GET /account/deletion-preview`, `POST /account/delete` (§5.7.1), `GET /account/preferences`, `PUT /account/preferences` (§4.7) |
| Devices | `GET /devices`, `DELETE /devices/{id}` |
| People | `GET /members` (the caller and the people they share a vault with; public keys, signatures), `POST /invites` (any user, or only admins under the invite policy, §8.3; emailed) |
| Share invitations | `POST /vaults/{id}/share-invitations` (owner), `GET /share-invitations` (`received`, `sent`, `waiting`), `POST /share-invitations/{id}/accept`, `POST /share-invitations/{id}/decline` (recipient), `DELETE /share-invitations/{id}` (owner) |
| Organizations (§5.11) | `GET /organizations`, `POST /organizations`, `GET /organizations/{org}`, `PUT /organizations/{org}/name`, `DELETE /organizations/{org}`, `POST /organizations/{org}/invitations`, `DELETE /organizations/{org}/invitations/{id}`, `POST /organization-invitations/{id}/accept`, `POST /organization-invitations/{id}/decline`, `PUT`/`DELETE /organizations/{org}/members/{accountID}`, `POST /organizations/{org}/teams`, `PUT /organizations/{org}/teams/{team}/name`, `DELETE /organizations/{org}/teams/{team}`, `PUT`/`DELETE /organizations/{org}/teams/{team}/members/{accountID}`, `PUT`/`DELETE /organizations/{org}/teams/{team}/vaults/{vaultID}`, `GET /vault-key-requests` (`pending`, `waiting`) |
| Vaults | `GET /vaults`, `POST /vaults`, `GET /vaults/{id}/members`, `PUT /vaults/{id}/access/{accountID}`, `DELETE /vaults/{id}/access/{accountID}` (an owner removes someone, or a member leaves with their own ID), `POST /vaults/{id}/rotate` (Phase 2), `PUT /vaults/{id}/name` and `POST /vaults/{id}/delete` (owner, §5.10) |
| Items | `GET /vaults/{id}/changes`, `PUT /vaults/{id}/items/{itemID}`, `GET /vaults/{id}/items/{itemID}/history`, `POST /vaults/{id}/items/{itemID}/move` (an owner or editor of both vaults: one transaction leaves a tombstone, sealed under the source key, in the source vault and writes the item, sealed again by the app under the target key with the same ID, to the target vault; 409 with the current item when `baseRevision` is stale, or when the target has a live item with that ID; 409 `{error, keyChanged: true}` when a part's `keyGeneration` is not its vault's current one, and 400 when a part names none for an organization vault, §7.3; both vaults' change feeds and events follow) |
| Attachments | `PUT/GET /vaults/{id}/attachments/{attID}/chunks/{n}` |
| Share links (§5.9) | `POST /share-links`, `GET /share-links`, `DELETE /share-links/{id}`; without a session `GET /public/share-links/{id}` (counts a view). The recipient page is `GET /s/{id}`, outside `/api/v1` |
| Approvals (§9.9) | `POST /approvals`, `GET /approvals` (pending), `GET /approvals/{id}`, `POST /approvals/{id}/answer`, `DELETE /approvals/{id}` (resolve; the requesting device only) |
| Desktop updates | `GET /desktop/updates/appcast.xml` (Sparkle appcast), `GET /desktop/updates/{version}/{file}` (streams a release's update archive); any signed-in session, 404 when desktop updates are off (§9.1.1) |

| App versions (§8.7) | `GET /version-check` (no session): 204 when the server accepts the caller's version, 426 otherwise |
| Admin (§5.1) | `GET /admin/server` (the public URL, for Overview), `GET /admin/health` and `POST /admin/health/secrets-backup` (§8.8), `GET`/`PUT /admin/settings` and `POST /admin/settings/test-email` (§8.3), `GET`/`POST /admin/invites`, `DELETE /admin/invites/{id}` and `POST /admin/invites/{id}/resend` (§5.1), `GET`/`POST /admin/setup/checks` and `POST /admin/setup/invite-policy` (the setup steps, §5.1), `GET /admin/accounts?query=&cursor=`, `GET /admin/accounts/{id}`, `POST /admin/accounts/{id}/suspend`, `POST /admin/accounts/{id}/reactivate`, `PUT /admin/accounts/{id}/role` and `POST /admin/accounts/{id}/delete` (with a re-authentication proof), `POST /admin/accounts/{id}/sign-out` and `DELETE /admin/accounts/{id}/devices/{deviceID}` (§5.1 Accounts), `GET /admin/audit`, `GET /admin/audit/types` and `GET /admin/audit.csv` (§8.3). Every route under `/admin` needs a session of an `admin` account and answers 403 `admin only` to any other |
| Setup (§5.1) | `GET /setup` (no session): `{"state"}`, one of `needsAdmin`, `needsChecks`, `needsInvitePolicy` and `done` |

All under `/api/v1`. JSON bodies, base64url for binary fields. There is no recovery API (§6). Outside `/api/v1` the server serves the pages `GET /s/{id}` (§5.9), `GET /invite/{code}` (§5.1), the admin dashboard `GET /admin` (§5.1), `GET /privacy` and `GET /terms` (§8.6).

### 8.3 Hardening

- Rate limiting per IP and per email on `prelogin` and `login` endpoints. `prelogin` returns a deterministic fake salt for unknown emails so it doesn't reveal which accounts exist.
- No account enumeration: `POST /invites`, `POST /vaults/{id}/share-invitations` and `POST /organizations/{org}/invitations` answer the same whether or not the email has an account, and do the account-dependent work (events, invites, email) after answering. All are rate-limited per account and per IP (§5.1, §5.8, §5.11).
- Request size limits. Strict JSON decoding (unknown fields rejected).
- Security headers on the invite page and the share link page (CSP, HSTS, no framing, §5.1, §5.9).
- Instance settings. Admins edit them in the dashboard under **Settings**: SMTP (**Email**, with **Send test email…**, which sends one message with the saved settings and shows the SMTP server's error; each admin may send 5 at once, then 5 a minute, and is answered 429 beyond that), the invite policy (**Sign-up**), the minimum app versions (**App versions**, §8.7), and the session lifetime (1–365 days, default 30), the auth burst (sign-in requests one IP may make before it is limited to one every 6 s; at least 1, no upper limit, default 10) and the retention of item history and expired invitations (1–3650 days) and of the audit log (30–3650 days), defaults in §8.6, under **Security**.
  - **Storage.** `instance_settings` holds one JSON document (`server/internal/settings`): `{"smtp": {"host", "port", "username", "password", "from"}, "minimumVersions": {"desktopMacOS", "iOS", "android", "cli", "extension"}, "invitePolicy": "anyone" | "admins", "sessionLifetimeDays", "authBurst", "retention": {"historyDays", "auditDays", "expiredInvitesDays"}, "setupState": "needsAdmin" | "needsChecks" | "needsInvitePolicy" | "done"}`. Every field is optional: a missing one keeps its default, and fields this version does not know are kept. `setupState` is the setup step of §5.1: only the setup endpoints move it, under the same row lock, and **Settings** neither shows nor edits it; a document without it starts at `needsAdmin` on a server without accounts and at `done` otherwise. The SMTP password an admin saves is stored in the database in plain text, not encrypted, as the server needs it to authenticate, so anyone who can read the database or its backups (§8.4) can read it. Setting Email with the `HISAR_SMTP_*` variables, `HISAR_SMTP_PASSWORD` among them, keeps it out of the database. `GET /admin/settings` answers only `{"set": true|false}` for it.
  - **The SMTP password stays with its server.** A save that changes `smtp.host`, `smtp.port` or `smtp.username` without setting `smtp.password` clears the stored password, so an admin cannot send the saved password to a host they choose; with a username, such a save is refused until the password is entered again ("Enter the SMTP password for the username. It must be entered again whenever the host, port or username changes."). The dashboard says so under **Password** as soon as one of those fields changes.
  - **Precedence.** A `HISAR_*` variable (§8.4) wins over the stored value, which wins over the default. Email is one unit: once any `HISAR_SMTP_*` variable is set, every Email field comes from the environment (a variable left empty gives the default, never the stored value), so a password from the environment is never combined with a host from the dashboard. `GET /admin/settings` answers each field's value, its source (`default`, `database` or `environment`) and its variable; the dashboard shows a field set by the environment read-only with "Set by HISAR_SMTP_HOST" (and Email with "Email is set by HISAR_SMTP_* variables on the server."), and `PUT` refuses to change it ("Email is set by HISAR_SMTP_* variables on the server, so it cannot be changed here." for an Email field). One `Validate` holds every bound; the server refuses to start when the effective settings fail it.
  - **Applying.** `PUT /admin/settings` changes only the fields it names, in one transaction on the locked row, then swaps one atomic snapshot that every request loads once; nothing needs a restart. A new auth burst applies to the per-IP limiter at once, the session lifetime to the next request of every session (sessions slide), and the retention to the next daily prune. The audit log records `admin_settings_changed` with the names of the changed fields, never their values. Each server process keeps its own snapshot, so a server with several processes picks up a change in the others only when they restart.
  - **Invite policy.** `anyone` (the default) or `admins`. Under `admins`, `POST /invites` from a member answers 403 "Only admins can invite people to the Hisar server.", which the Mac and iPhone apps and `hisar invite` through the app show as it is. A share invitation or organization invitation to an email without an account still answers 202 exactly as before, so it still reveals nothing; the server just creates and emails no sign-up invite for it, and the invitation waits until that person gets an account some other way.
- Audit log for logins, device changes, invites (`invite` from the apps, `operator_invite` from `hisar-server invite`, and `admin_invite_created`, `admin_invite_revoked` and `admin_invite_resent` from the admin dashboard, §5.1), share invitations, sharing changes, credential changes, failed re-authentications, account deletions, renaming, deleting and leaving vaults (`vault_renamed`, `vault_deleted` with the member count and the target vault and item count of a move, `vault_left`; IDs only), organization changes (§5.11), setup (§5.1: `admin_settings_changed` for the invite policy, then `setup_completed`), and admins' changes to other accounts (§5.1 Accounts, with `detail.account`), kept 1 year (§8.6).
  - **Types.** `server/internal/service/audit.go` holds every type the server records in one table, with its group and the label the dashboard shows; the service records only keys of that table, never a string, and a test checks that every key has its row. Stored names never change.
  - **Reading it.** Admins read the log in the dashboard under **Audit log**. `GET /admin/audit?account=&type=&group=&from=&to=&cursor=` answers `{events, nextCursor}`: 100 events a page, newest first, paged by `(created_at, id)`, so no event repeats from one page to the next. `account` is an email (the account that has it now) or an account ID, which also finds a deleted account's events; such events show the ID and no email (`accountEmail` is null). `type` names one type and `group` all types of a group (`accounts`, `vaults`, `sharing`, `organizations`, `admin`), not both; `from` (inclusive) and `to` (exclusive) are RFC 3339 times. `GET /admin/audit/types` lists the groups and types for the filter.
  - **Export.** `GET /admin/audit.csv` with the same filters answers `text/csv` as an attachment. The server reads it from the database 500 rows at a time, freeing the connection between batches, and sends each batch as it goes, so its memory does not grow with the log; the dashboard saves the whole file from browser memory. If the database fails partway, the server closes the connection before the last chunk, so the browser reports a failed download instead of a short file. Columns: ID, Time (UTC), Event (the label), Type, Account ID, Account email, Device ID, IP address, Details (the JSON detail). A field a spreadsheet would run as a formula (starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or carriage return) gets a leading `'`. The audit log records `admin_audit_exported` with the filters of the export.

### 8.4 Deployment

- Single static binary `hisar-server` in a Docker image, with Postgres 17 alongside it in Docker Compose.
- The image builds the admin dashboard (§5.1) before `go build` embeds it: `hisar-admin-wasm` with `GOOS=js GOARCH=wasm`, then the Vite build in a `node:24` stage.
- Schema migrations: goose (`pressly/goose`), SQL files embedded in the binary, up and down sections, applied on start under a Postgres advisory lock or explicitly with `hisar-server migrate up|down|status`.
- HTTP framework: Fiber v3 (fasthttp). It serves plain HTTP behind the reverse proxy; HTTP/2 and TLS are handled by the reverse proxy (nginx in the reference deployment).
- Served publicly over https behind nginx, which terminates TLS. A run of the GitHub Actions workflow "Release" with the app `server` (README.md, "CI and releases") tags `server-<version>`, and its workflow "Release server image" builds `deploy/Dockerfile` for amd64 and arm64 and pushes it to the GitHub Container Registry as `ghcr.io/tosunlab/hisar-server:<version>` and `:sha-<commit>`, built with that version (`-X main.version` from the image's `VERSION` build argument; `make docker VERSION=1.2.3` does the same locally, and a build without a tag is `sha-<commit>`); the run then creates the GitHub prerelease `server-<version>`. Its Publish job, once approved, points `:latest` and `:<major>.<minor>` at it and turns the prerelease into the latest release, which is what admin dashboards offer as a newer server (§8.8). A custom build (`server-1.2.3-label`) can't be published.
- Attachments on local disk (later: S3-compatible storage).
- Instance settings (§8.3) are edited in the admin dashboard; these variables override them field by field, except Email, which any `HISAR_SMTP_*` variable takes over as a whole, and the dashboard then shows the field (or all of Email) read-only.
- Optional email (§8.5): `HISAR_SMTP_HOST`, `HISAR_SMTP_PORT` (default 587), `HISAR_SMTP_USERNAME`, `HISAR_SMTP_PASSWORD` and `HISAR_SMTP_FROM`. With a host, the effective settings need a port, a sender address and, with a username, a password; the server refuses to start otherwise. Setting them keeps the SMTP password out of the database (§8.3).
- `HISAR_AUTH_BURST`: the auth burst of §8.3.
- Desktop update source (§9.1.1): `HISAR_DESKTOP_UPDATE_GITHUB_REPOSITORY` (default `tosunlab/hisar` from `core/project`; `off` turns updates off and the update endpoints answer 404), the optional `HISAR_DESKTOP_UPDATE_GITHUB_TOKEN` (a fine-grained token with read-only Contents access to that repository, needed while it is private) and `HISAR_DESKTOP_UPDATE_GITHUB_API_URL` (default `https://api.github.com`, for GitHub Enterprise and tests). The same repository's `server-<version>` releases tell the admin dashboard about a newer server (§8.8); `off` turns that off too.
- Optional minimum app versions (§8.7): `HISAR_MINIMUM_VERSION_DESKTOP_MACOS`, `HISAR_MINIMUM_VERSION_IOS`, `HISAR_MINIMUM_VERSION_ANDROID`, `HISAR_MINIMUM_VERSION_CLI` and `HISAR_MINIMUM_VERSION_EXTENSION`, each a semantic version such as `0.1.6`; empty leaves the dashboard's value, which is no minimum by default. The server refuses to start with a value that is not a version.
- Nightly `pg_dump` + attachments encrypted with `age` and copied off-server. Restore tested quarterly.

### 8.5 Email

`server/internal/mail` has a small `Mailer` interface with an SMTP implementation and a recording fake for tests.

- **Transport.** Port 465 uses implicit TLS; any other port must offer STARTTLS, which is used before authenticating. Plaintext is refused, so credentials never cross the network unencrypted; the only exception is a loopback host, which the end-to-end test's SMTP sink uses (`server/cmd/hisar-smtp-sink`). Authentication is PLAIN over TLS. Each message has a 30 s deadline.
- **Messages.** Plain text in UTF-8, no HTML, no images and no tracking; short and written for developers. Names from users are stripped of control characters before they go into a header.
  - Invite: who invited them, the sign-up link (the invite page, §5.1: "Get Hisar and create your account") and the invite code ("Or paste this invite code under Create account in Hisar"), the expiry, and that the email can be ignored.
  - Share invitation to an account: "<Name> shared a vault with you in Hisar", the role, and where to accept (Settings → People, `hisar invitations`).
  - Share invitation without an account: the same, plus the sign-up link and code.
  - Share accepted, to the inviter (§5.8, sent only if their app has not completed it after 2 minutes): "<Name> accepted your invitation in Hisar", that the server cannot say which vault, and to open and unlock Hisar on a device to finish sharing.
  - Master password changed or Secret Key replaced (§5.7), to the account: when, how many other devices were signed out and how to sign in on them, and to contact support if it was not them.
  - Account deleted (§5.7.1), to the deleted address: what was deleted, what stays with other owners, and the backup and audit log retention.
- Sending happens in the background after the API answered; failures are logged without the recipient's address.

### 8.6 Privacy policy and Terms

`GET /privacy` and `GET /terms` serve two static pages embedded in `hisar-server` (`privacy.html`, `terms.html`), linked from each other, from the store listings and from the desktop app's create-account screen. They describe the hosted service at `api.tryhisar.com` exactly as this document does: what the server stores (§2.3, §8.1), what it cannot read, the sub-processors (Hetzner Online GmbH in Helsinki, Finland, for hosting; Google for email) and the retention below. The operator is Alper Berber, the contact is support@tryhisar.com, and the terms are under Dutch law. Both pages get the share link page's headers minus scripts: `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; frame-ancestors 'none'`, cacheable for an hour.

**Retention**, as the pages state it. These are the defaults of the instance settings (§8.3); an operator may change them in the admin dashboard under **Settings → Security**, and the hosted service keeps them, because the privacy page states them:

- Account data, devices, vaults, items and invitations: while the account exists. Users delete their account in the apps or the CLI (§5.7.1), or by emailing support; an admin of the Hisar server can delete it from the admin dashboard (§5.1). Item history: 90 days. Share links: deleted at expiry, after the last view or when revoked (§5.9). A share invitation: until the share is completed, declined or cancelled; an organization invitation: until it is accepted, declined or cancelled (§5.11). Sign-up invites, pending share invitations and organization invitations that were not used: `hisar-server` deletes them once a day when they expired more than 30 days ago (accepted share invitations do not expire; they wait for the owner's app).
- Audit log (`audit_events`: account, device, event type, IP address and details: the device's name and platform for a login, the email for a sign-up, an invite, a share invitation or an organization invitation, otherwise IDs only): 1 year; `hisar-server` deletes older rows once a day.
- Rate-limit counters, the event broker and phone approvals: server memory only, never written to disk, gone on restart (approvals after at most 4 minutes).
- The reverse proxy's access logs: 14 days. The encrypted backups (§8.4): 30 days.

### 8.7 Minimum app versions

The operator can require a minimum version per app, in the admin dashboard under **Settings → App versions** or with `HISAR_MINIMUM_VERSION_*` (§8.4), mostly for API compatibility: an app below it must update before it can be used. The minimum is independent of the latest version the update feed offers (§9.1.1); raising it is an explicit, rare decision.

- **Headers.** Every app sends `Hisar-Platform` and `Hisar-Version` with every request, the event stream included. The platform is the one the device registers with: `darwin/desktop` (Mac app), `ios/mobile` (iPhone app), `android/mobile` (Android app), `<os>/cli` (`hisar`, any OS); the desktop app checks the browser extension and an app-mode `hisar` on their behalf with `browser/extension` and `<os>/cli` (below). Development builds (`development`, or no bundle version) send neither header.
- **Middleware.** `requireMinimumVersion` runs first on every `/api/v1` request, before rate limits, sessions and the handlers, so sign-in, sign-up and `GET /version-check` answer it too and a signed-out app knows. When the platform has a minimum and the version is below it, the answer is `426 Upgrade Required` with `{"error": "update required", "minimumVersion", "updateURL"}`; `updateURL` is `https://tryhisar.com/download` for the Mac, iPhone, Android and `hisar`, and the Chrome Web Store page for the extension.
- **What passes:** requests without either header, from platforms without a minimum (Linux desktop, other callers), and versions that do not parse. So very old apps from before the headers, scripts and other callers keep working; only an app that says it is too old is refused. `/api/v1/desktop/updates/…` always passes, so a refused Mac app can still update itself through Sparkle. The headers are the caller's own claim, so the minimum only tells honest apps to update; it is never an access control, and a protocol fix that must hold for every caller is enforced by the server on the request itself.
- **Versions** are semantic versions compared by `core/appversion` (also the updater's parser, `desktopupdate.GetBuildNumber`): a pre-release comes before its release (`1.0.0-alpha-test` < `1.0.0`; a custom build `1.2.3-label` is below a minimum of `1.2.3`), build metadata is ignored.
- **Clients.** `core/apiclient` sends the headers when `Client.Version` is set and turns a 426 into `*apiclient.UpgradeRequiredError` (not "unreachable", so the offline cache never hides it), calling `Client.OnUpgradeRequired`.
  - **Mac app:** the backend records the requirement from any answer (`Status.updateRequired`: current version, minimum, update URL), stops the event stream and emits `update-required`, which shows the window. The app also asks `GET /version-check` at launch. The UI then shows only a blocking "Update required" screen: "This server needs Hisar 0.2.0 or later. This Mac has 0.1.0.", **Update now** (Sparkle when it is available and signed in, otherwise the download page) and **Check again** (any answer but 426 clears the requirement, e.g. after the operator lowered the minimum). Meanwhile the SSH agent (signing and listing keys, which also shows the screen), the command-line socket (every request but `hello`, coding agents included) and the browser bridge (every paired request) answer at once with "Hisar needs an update: the server requires version 0.2.0 or later…" instead of waiting.
  - **iPhone app:** the same requirement through `hisarkit` (`SetAppVersion` right after `New`, `CheckMinimumVersion`, `EventListener.OnUpdateRequired`), checked at launch and on returning to the foreground; a blocking "Update required" screen with **Install the latest Hisar** (the download page until TestFlight or the App Store exist) and **Check again**. The AutoFill extension says Hisar needs an update instead of offering logins.
  - **Android app:** the same calls and screen as the iPhone, with the minimum from **Android app** in the dashboard or `HISAR_MINIMUM_VERSION_ANDROID`. The version is `hisar.versionName` in `mobile/android/gradle.properties`, which debug builds send too. "This device has 0.1.0." ends the screen's sentence. While an update is required, AutoFill offers only "Unlock Hisar", and its screen says "Hisar needs an update. Open Hisar to update it."
  - **hisar:** standalone, a 426 prints `hisar: update required: the Hisar server needs hisar 1.0.0 or later, and this is 0.9.0.` with where to download it, and exits with 1. In app mode it sends `clientPlatform` and `clientVersion` with every request; the app refuses with `update-required` when the app itself needs an update or when the server's `GET /version-check` refuses that `hisar` (asked by the app, cached for an hour).
  - **Browser extension:** `hello` carries `extensionVersion`; the app answers `updateRequired: {target: "app" | "extension", currentVersion, minimumVersion, updateURL}` (the extension's version is checked like `hisar`'s). The popup shows "Hisar needs an update" with **Open Hisar**, or "Update the Hisar extension" with **Update extension**; while the extension is too old it refuses fills itself, and the inline menu points to the popup.
- **Offline:** without an answer from the server nothing is refused and the cache keeps working; the check applies on the next contact. A requirement already known stays until the server accepts the version again or the app restarts (a restart checks again at launch).

### 8.8 Server health

**Overview** in the admin dashboard (§5.1) reads `GET /api/v1/admin/health`: `{version, latestVersion, database: {ok, migrations}, counts: {accounts, suspendedAccounts, vaults, items, shareLinks, pendingInvites}, storageBytes, checks}`.

- **Version.** `version` is the release the binary was built from (§8.4), `dev` for a local build. `latestVersion` is the highest `server-MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` release of the update repository (§8.4) when it is newer than `version`, read once an hour and skipping drafts, prereleases and custom builds; it is empty otherwise, for a version that does not parse, and when updates are `off`.
- **Database.** `ok` when every migration is applied; each migration has its name and when it was applied. `storageBytes` is `pg_database_size(current_database())`. The counts are every account and the suspended ones among them (§5.1 Accounts), live items, share links that have not expired and sign-up invites neither used nor expired other than the setup invite, the ones **Invites** lists (§5.1). Counting items reads the whole items table, so one count runs at a time (requests that arrive meanwhile wait for it) and is reused for a minute: the item count may be up to a minute old, which Overview says under Usage. When the reads after the session lookup fail or take over 8 seconds, the answer is 503 `the server cannot reach its database`; so is every session lookup that fails on the database, so apps treat an outage as an unreachable server and stay signed in.
- **Checks.** One table in `server/internal/service` (`RunInstanceChecks`), `ok`, `warning` or `failed` with a sentence each, run at once. The setup page's checks step (§5.1) runs the same table through `GET`/`POST /admin/setup/checks` and offers **I have a backup** there too.
  - `public-url`: the server fetches `<HISAR_PUBLIC_URL>/healthz` (5 s, no redirects) and expects `ok`. Failed when `HISAR_PUBLIC_URL` is not an http or https URL, or when the URL answers anything else, so it leads to another server. When the fetch does not get through (DNS, connection, timeout), a warning instead: in Docker or behind a router without hairpin NAT a server cannot reach its own public URL even when it works, so the message says to open it from another device.
  - `https`: `HISAR_PUBLIC_URL` uses https, or http on a loopback host (`localhost` or a loopback address, `mail.IsLoopbackHost`, the rule invite and email links follow); otherwise failed.
  - `proxy`: warns when the request has `X-Forwarded-For` without `HISAR_TRUST_PROXY`, or the reverse.
  - `migrations`: failed while a migration is pending or the status cannot be read; reading it takes no migration lock.
  - `secrets-backup`: warns "Back up hisar-secrets.json. Nobody can sign in without it." until an admin clicks **I have a backup** on Overview or in setup. `POST /admin/health/secrets-backup` stores the time as `secretsBackupConfirmedAt` in the instance settings document (§8.3), which **Settings** neither shows nor edits, swaps the snapshot, records `admin_secrets_backup_confirmed` and answers the check, now ok; it stays ok across restarts.
  - `email`: warns "Email is not set up. Invites from the apps will fail." without SMTP (§8.5).

## 9. Clients

All clients use `core/` for crypto, the local store and sync.

### 9.1 Desktop — Wails v3 (Go + Vue/TypeScript)

- The Go side owns the unlocked keyset and vault keys (in `memguard` locked buffers). The web UI calls Go methods and receives only the decrypted data it asks for.
- Local store: SQLite (`modernc.org/sqlite`, pure Go) holding the server's ciphertext as-is plus sync state. Nothing is stored decrypted on disk.
  - `core/cache` stores the account record (salt, Argon2 params, `encKeyset`, public keys), the vault list as the server returns it (wrapped keys, encrypted names), every live item's encrypted parts, each vault's `seq` and the sealed account preferences record with the favorites (§4.7). The file is `desktop-cache.db` next to `desktop.json`, mode 0600, and is deleted on sign-in, sign-up and sign-out.
  - Remotely fetched accounts replace the cache only after keyset verification. `AttachCache` also compares the fetched credential envelope with the one established by signup, unlock, or a committed credential change and checks public keys against the open keyset. A second account fetch cannot overwrite the last verified offline envelope with a forged one.
  - `session.Session.Cache`: listing vaults replaces the cached list and forgets vaults that disappeared (removed access). Listing items asks for changes since the cached `seq`, applies them page by page in one transaction each (tombstones delete the row), and decrypts from the cache. A `nextSeq` below the cached `seq` is a rollback (§7.5). A cached item that no longer decrypts makes the vault download from `seq` 0 once.
  - The server counts as unreachable on a connection error or a 502/503/504 from the reverse proxy (`client.IsUnreachable`). Then reads, unlock with the master password, Touch ID unlock and the master password check come from the cache, and the session reports `IsOffline`. Any other error, such as 401 for a revoked device, is never hidden by the cache.
  - Offline, the app shows an "Offline" notice and refuses changes with a clear message. Edits are not queued; that would need the merge of §7.4. Background sync runs every 60 s and when the window gets focus, and stays quiet while an item is being edited.
- Auto-lock after idle time (chosen in Settings: 30 seconds, 1 minute, 5 minutes, 10 minutes by default, or 1 hour; a device preference kept across sign-out) and on screen lock/sleep. Clipboard cleared after 90 s, or when Hisar locks or the desktop app quits before then, in each case only while it still holds Hisar's copy (on Android the lock clear sees the clipboard only while Hisar is in front; the 90 s alarm still clears a copy made before a lock in the background).
  - **Idle means the Mac is idle.** On macOS the idle time is the time since the last keyboard, mouse or trackpad input anywhere on the Mac (`CGEventSourceSecondsSinceLastEventType(kCGEventSourceStateHIDSystemState, kCGAnyInputEventType)`, `desktop/systemidle`), not only in Hisar's window. So a developer typing in a terminal or editor keeps Hisar unlocked, and the approvals they gave ("Don't ask again for 1 hour", remembered commit signing, command-line sessions) last as long as promised; a Mac nobody touches locks after the chosen time even while a script, coding agent or remembered approval keeps using Hisar. Requests (SSH agent signatures, the command-line tool, coding agents, browser fills) therefore never postpone auto-lock themselves: counting them would let unattended automation keep Hisar unlocked forever. The timer fires at the chosen time after the last reset and checks the system idle time; when the Mac saw input since, it waits for the rest of the interval and checks again.
  - On Linux the idle time comes from the session bus: GNOME's `org.gnome.Mutter.IdleMonitor.GetIdletime`, else `org.freedesktop.ScreenSaver.GetSessionIdleTime` (KDE, Xfce and others), each call limited to 500 ms; a desktop that answers neither is not asked again for a minute. That call answers seconds, except on KDE, whose screen locker answers milliseconds; KDE is recognized by the same connection owning `org.kde.screensaver`. Hisar dials its own session-bus connection (at most 2 s) and dials again once the bus drops it, so system idle comes back after a session-bus restart.
  - Where the system idle time is not available (a Linux desktop without either monitor, KDE on Wayland, which refuses, or a failed call), idle is measured as before: input in Hisar's window (`ReportActivity`) and calls into the vault backend from the UI or the command-line tool.
  - **Idle time counts sleep.** Go's timers stop while the device sleeps (golang/go#24595), so `core/app` also keeps the moment auto-lock is due on a clock that counts sleep (`CLOCK_BOOTTIME` on Linux and Android, `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` on macOS and iOS, the wall clock elsewhere), and every status read, fill and other use of the unlocked session first locks once that moment has passed; the timer only locks promptly while the device is awake. So a phone that slept past its auto-lock time never fills through AutoFill or Credential Manager without an unlock, whatever the app's lifecycle saw.
  - Screen lock and sleep still lock at once (`background`), whatever the idle time.
- Ships the native-messaging host for the browser extension and the `hisar` CLI, which can use the app's session (§9.4).
- **Settings** (⌘, or the gear) is a window-like dialog, about 880 × 620 points: a source list on the left with a **Search settings** field, the selected section on the right with its rows in rounded groups. Sections, in this order:
  - **General**: Appearance and Color (§9.10); Open Hisar at login (§9.1.2); the version with **Check for updates** and **Automatically check for updates** (§9.1.1); Import from another password manager (§9.7).
  - **Security**: Unlock with Touch ID, Auto-lock, the Emergency Kit with the Secret Key, **Change master password…** and **Replace Secret Key…** (§5.7).
  - **SSH & Git**: the SSH agent's status with **Use Hisar for SSH** and the `~/.ssh/config` section folded under a disclosure, **Remember SSH approvals** (Up to 1 hour by default, or Until Hisar locks; §9.6), Compact SSH approvals, Approve requests on my smart device, and **Remember commit signing** (Always ask by default, or up to 10 minutes or 1 hour; §9.6).
  - **Developer tools**: the `hisar` command-line tool (install, PATH, Let the command-line tool use Hisar, §9.4) and Coding agents (`hisar mcp` and the per-vault "Available to agents" switch, §9.4.2).
  - **Browser**: browsers the extension is ready in, paired browsers with Remove, Only fill on the exact host (§9.2).
  - **People**: invitations for you (the source list shows their number as a badge), people with their security codes, invitations you sent, and **Invite to Hisar** in the header (§5.1), which explains that it creates a Hisar account and that vaults are shared from the vault's Share panel. Sharing a vault is **Invite by email** with **Share** in that panel (§5.8).
  - **Organizations**: invitations to organizations (counted in the badge), your organizations, **Create organization…**, and each organization's members, teams, team vaults and admin actions (§5.11 Screens).
  - **Devices**: signed-in devices with last activity, Revoke.
  - **Export**: "Export your data" (§9.11): **Encrypted export (recommended)** with the export passphrase entered twice, or **Plain JSON** behind a warning box; **Export with Touch ID…** (or the master password field and **Export…** without Touch ID) opens a save dialog and says "Exported N items from M vaults to <path>."
  - **Account**: name, email, server, Sign out and **Delete account…** (§5.7.1).
  - **Help**: **Contact support** with the address `support@tryhisar.com` as selectable text and **Copy** (one constant, `desktop/frontend/src/support.ts`), and **Diagnostics**: the Hisar version and build, the macOS version, the server URL and this Mac's device ID, with **Copy diagnostics**. Diagnostics never include secrets, names or other people's emails. The server does not report its version, and there is no public documentation URL yet, so neither is shown.
  - Search matches row titles, descriptions and keywords: the source list keeps only sections with a match, the selected section shows only its matching rows, and no match says so. ↑/↓ move between sections, ⌘F focuses the search, Esc closes. The last selected section is per-device UI state (the web view's local storage) and opens again next time.
- **Favorites** (§4.7): the first entry of the sidebar, above Vaults, with the number of favorites. It lists the starred items of every vault sorted by title, each with its vault's name, and searches like a vault; opening one shows it with its vault's actions (a reader's item stays read-only). Empty, it says "No favorites yet" and how to add one, and that only the user sees them. The star in the item's header (**Add to Favorites**, **Remove from Favorites**), ⌘D for the selected item and the item's context menu in any list toggle it; starred items show a small star in a vault's list. The list follows other devices live (§7.2).
- **Item menu**: right-clicking an item in any list opens a menu built from what applies to it: **Open**; **Copy username**, **Copy password**, **Copy one-time code** and the item's main value (card number, license key, API secret, a database's connection string, an SSH key's public key), which appear once the item's details load from the local cache, since list summaries carry no secrets; **Open website** (**Open admin console** for a server); **Add to Favorites** or **Remove from Favorites**; **Share link…** (once the details load, for an item with something to share), which opens the item and its share link dialog (§5.9); and, where the item's vault is writable, **Edit**, **Duplicate** (not for SSH keys), **Move to…** (when another vault is writable) and **Delete…**. **Duplicate** saves "GitHub copy" in the same vault with everything but its passkeys, since one passkey can't live in two items, and opens it. **Move to…** asks for a vault the user may add items to and moves the item there with `POST /vaults/{id}/items/{itemID}/move` (§8.2): it keeps its ID, so it stays a favorite and ID-based references still find it, while `hisar://` references by vault name need the new name; a server keeps its SSH key link, which shows as missing until a key from the new vault is chosen. Copying uses the item's own vault, so a favorite from another vault copies without being opened. **Delete…** asks "Delete “GitHub”?" (removed from all the user's devices, or from the vault for everyone with access) before deleting. Right-clicking a field row in an item's details offers that row's own buttons by name (Reveal or Hide, Copy, Copy secret reference, Open); with text selected in the row, the system menu appears instead. Menus take the arrow keys and Escape, and stay inside the window.
- **Item icons** (§4.5): the editor's tile is a **Change icon** button opening a picker: **Automatic** (the website icon, or the initials or the category's icon), the 30 presets, a row of colors and **Upload image…** (PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP, prepared by `PrepareItemIcon` and previewed before saving). Tiles in the list, Favorites, the detail panel and the editor draw the chosen image or preset, else a login's website icon, which `ItemTile` asks `GetWebsiteIcon` for once the tile is on screen, with at most four requests at a time and one per domain per session; until it arrives the initials stay, so the list never moves.
- **Vault menu** (§5.10): right-clicking a vault in the sidebar, or **⋯** (Vault actions) in the item list's header, offers **Rename…** (owners), **Delete vault…** and **Leave vault…** (shared vaults). An action the user may not take opens a short dialog with the reason (for the Personal vault: why it can't be deleted) instead of a disabled item. **Delete “Work”?** lists "These people lose access:" for a shared vault and, when it has items, offers "Move its N items to [vault]" (the default) or "Delete the vault and its N items", which needs "Type Work to confirm" before **Delete vault** enables. **Leave “Work”?** says the user loses access to its items on all their devices and that an owner can invite them again. Afterwards the app selects the target vault (or Personal) and shows "Moved N items to Personal and deleted Work.", "Deleted Work." or "You left Work."
- Runs the SSH agent (§9.6) and handles Touch ID (§5.4).
- **Sign in and create account**: one tagline on both tabs ("Passwords, SSH keys and secrets for developers, end-to-end encrypted.") with factual bullets: sign-in shows the SSH agent (private keys never leave Hisar), the CLI (reads passwords and one-time codes and uses SSH keys through the agent) and end-to-end encryption; create account shows breach safety, the two secrets and the Emergency Kit. The Server field is behind **Use a different server…** (§5.3), and the account form has the strength meter (§5.2).

#### 9.1.1 Automatic updates (macOS)

The app updates itself with [Sparkle 2](https://sparkle-project.org) through the user's own Hisar server, so the app never talks to a third party and a server's users only get builds its operator allows.
